- #1276 — `api/src/notebooks_api.jl:50` — `_notebook_server_alive` uses a bare `catch → false` around an `HTTP.get` probe, then drives `_ensure_notebook_server!`'s launch decision — the exact shape the `_preview_ping` fix narrowed. Any code bug in the probe (renamed helper, wrong kwarg, an `UndefVarError` from a name collision inside `Main`) is silently reported as "not alive", triggering a duplicate-launch loop identical to the one the preview fix undoes. - #1276 — `app/src/preview.jl:140` — `launch!(w::PreviewWorker)` ping-wait loop, shape A, bare `catch` with no exception variable swallows every exception from `send(w, Dict("type" => "ping"))` — the same worker send/ping path the just-fixed `_preview_ping` guards, only during initial startup instead of steady state. A `MethodError` from an `HTTP.WebSockets.send` refactor, a `JSON3` shape drift on the reply, or any code-shape bug in `send`/`_recv_json` becomes indistinguishable from "worker not up yet" and produces the terminal "Preview worker did not start within 90 seconds" (or falsely blames a squatter) instead of surfacing the real error. Same fix shape: narrow to `Base.IOError | HTTP.ConnectError | HTTP.WebSockets.WebSocketError` and rethrow the rest. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1276 — `app/src/runner/client.jl:60` — `runner_ping` — exact same alive-probe shape as pre-fix `_preview_ping`: `try; _runner_get(h, "/ping"; timeout); catch; nothing; end`. Consumed by `runner_alive` (line 63) and `runner_launch!` (line 83) to decide adopt-vs-relaunch, exactly the launch-decision path this diff is repairing on the preview side. A non-network throw inside `_runner_get` (a `MethodError`/`ArgumentError` from an `HTTP.get` refactor, a `JSON3.read` shape drift) is swallowed as "nothing there" — and this file's own docstring at line 55-57 spells out the failure mode: "reporting 'nothing there' for a process that is very much there produces a relaunch loop against a port that can never be bound." Same case-F shape the diff pins narrower catches on for `_preview_ping` and `_notebook_server_alive`. [**fixed_pre_commit**]

- #1308 — `.claude/hooks/check_commit_recital.py:82` — `_FINDING_MARKERS` / `check()`. The hook counts every `**confirmed**` / `**should reuse**` in the commit message, including ones inside backtick code spans. It doesn't use the new `_outside_code` blanking. The recital appends reviewer output verbatim as evidence, so a checked-and-clear line that quotes the tag (the exact case in `test_marker_quoted_in_backticks_is_not_a_finding`) now gets no slug and no warning from the recital, yet the hook still counts it as a finding needing an outcome tag. Result: one marker, zero outcomes, and the commit is blocked. The two parsers now disagree about what a finding is. [**fixed_pre_commit**]

It leaves out the value columns each chart adds after `idH`: `x0`/`x1`/`count` (histogram), `category`/`count`/`value` (frequency), `mean`/`sd`/`sem`/`ci95`/`n` (bar), `percent`/`ci95_lower`/`ci95_upper`/`n_positive` (percent), `q1`/`median`/`q3`/`lower`/`upper` (boxplot) and `value` (points). - #1333 — `python/cecelia/writers/render_animation_run.py:292` — (`_draw_overlays2d`, called from `run`): the overlays are drawn after the sRGB encode. `_render_frame` now returns sRGB-encoded bytes, and `_draw_overlays2d` (lines 80 and 92) then paints the track segments and points with raw `int(r * 255)` colours on top. - #1335 — `api/src/image_geometry.jl:480` — the storage-modal `labels` size loop does `fns = v isa AbstractVector ? v : [v]` and then `string(fn)`. Adopted rows from `GET /api/tasks` carry another profile's `by` (`runningTasks.ts:154`) and can be re-run (`adopt()` docstring). When a later pass judges one `live_bug`, it becomes `open` with an older `first_seen`, so the owner queue never gets it. - #1397 — `scripts/claude_md_eval/supervise.py:240` — `judge_input` → `anti_lines` re-runs `anti_signal` line by line over the raw `additions`, but skips the `anti_signal_ignore_comments` handling that `_regex_hits` (`run_prompt.py:203-206`) applies. - #1424 — `app/src/tasks/segment/measure_labels.jl:78` — `segment.measureLabels` → `measure_utils._to_anndata` (`python/cecelia/utils/measure_utils.py:474`) rewrites `{vn}.h5ad` from scratch, the same way a re-track rebuilds `{vn}__tracks.h5ad`. - #1452 — `frontend/src/components/plots/GateOverlay.vue:248` — `exportSvgContent`, the SVG export of the gate outlines. The new `_include_edges` comment says a far gate edge is "left off-axis and the renderer clips the gate at the border". The live canvas and the PNG renderer do clip, but this export does not: it writes each `svgPolygon` at raw `dataToPx` coordinates. `GateScatterCell.vue:266` then places those polygons inside a plain `<g transform=translate>` with no `clipPath`. A rectangle drawn up to 5000 on data that reaches 10 would run across the axes in a single-plot SVG export, and into neighbouring tiles when montage tiles are stitched together. Before this change, plotmeta always grew the axes to cover every gate, so this path never received off-axis geometry [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1456 — `api/src/cell_cards_api.jl:153` — `partOf` read written out by hand: `String[string(x) for x in get(entry, "partOf", get(entry, :partOf, String[]))]` with `isempty(part_of) ? - #1484 — `frontend/src/components/ViewerPanel.vue:833` — `forceViewerResync` → `publishViewerCacheClear({ imageUid: uid })`. The new gate in the `onViewerCacheClear` handler (`ViewerWindow.vue:4965` `named`, then `shownStoresChanged`) only reloads when the event names the shown vn, or when a fingerprint or dim moved. A manual resync on an unchanged store therefore hits the in-place meta merge and `return`s before `cacheClearRev.value = ev.rev` and `reallocate()`. The button stops bumping `_r=` and no longer busts the browser HTTP cache, which is the one case it exists for. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1484 — `frontend/src/modules/ViewerWindow.vue:4081` — `watch(meta, …)` publishes `setLabelsDimMismatch` from `m.labelDims`. The new skip path in the cache-clear handler (≈4989) updates `prev.labelDims` / `prev.labelNames` in place and never replaces `meta.value`. A new segmentation made on another image version (different nX/nY) becomes tickable but is never flagged as mismatched. Before this change the handler always ran `meta.value = m`, which fired this watcher. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1486 — `docs/inventory/MCP.md:35` — the recommend-route contract. This line still calls it "the pure `POST /api/correction-plan/recommend`". The diff dropped "pure" from the same route in `mcp/cecelia_mcp/autonomous.py:50`, `mcp/tests/test_autonomous.py:71`, `docs/API.md:277` and the `correction_plan_api.jl` header, but missed this copy. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1488 — `api/src/viewer_api.jl:927` — `api_viewer_pick_cell` clamps the requested level to the mask's own pyramid depth (`clamp(lvl_req, 0, nlvl - 1)` on the `label_store_path` store). That is the clamp this diff replaced in `try_serve_slab`. The client sends `x`/`y` in coordinates for the level on screen (`screenToImagePx(..., renderNX, renderNY)`, `level: slabLevel.value`, ViewerWindow.vue:2862–2883). For a single-level mask under a zoomed-out view, the server reads mask L0 at level-N coordinates and picks a cell at the wrong position. This fix now makes such masks visible at that zoom, so users can now reach this path. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1488 — `api/src/viewer_api.jl:991` — `api_viewer_pick_rect` has the same mask-depth clamp. The rect corners come from `screenToImagePx` at `renderNX/renderNY` (ViewerWindow.vue:2914–2924), so for a single-level mask the rect reads the wrong region of mask L0 and returns the wrong set of labels. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1488 — `frontend/src/utils/brickLoader.ts:126` — `brickLabelSlabUrl`, the mask fetch for the 3D brick path, is a third caller of the `/api/viewer/slab?labels=` route, and `label_level_plan` now depends on which image version the request names. The hunk fixed this for the pick routes by adding `imageValueName`, and the flat-slab mask fetch at `ViewerWindow.vue:2051` already sends `valueName: valueName.value`. The brick URL's `base` type is `{projectUid, imageUid, enc?, rev?}`, which has no `valueName`/`version`, so the server's `vnn` is `nothing` and a fitted mask brick is sized against the ACTIVE version's level shape. A viewer on a non-active version whose dims differ, for example after drift-correction canvas expansion, gets a mask brick sized for the wrong grid. `fetchLabelBrick` allows a smaller nx/ny for edge bricks, so this may not be rejected. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1501 — `frontend/src/components/GpuDiagnostic.vue:108` — this is the Settings "GPU type" label for `GpuProbeReport.looksDiscrete`. It has the same flaw this diff fixes in the ViewerWindow Debug "Classified" row: when the name is blank it hard-codes `(from limits — the browser gives no adapter name)`. But `probeWebGpu` now classifies a blank-name adapter by `subgroupMinSize` first, so on the RTX A4500 case (blank name, limit 2048, subgroup 32) Settings would say "Discrete (from limits…)" when the limit actually points to integrated. The fix needs `classifyAdapterBy(...).by` here too. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1504 — `api/src/overlay_author.jl:577` — `_cb_prepare(df, …, m; scale = cb_scale)` on the track / trackclust path. Here `df` is `pop_df(img, pt, want_paths; …)`, which holds only the shown pops' cells. When one segmentation is drawn, `cb_scale` is `nothing` (`length(lvns) > 1` gate at :871). The fallback `_cb_scale_of([col], cb_col)` then decides the kind with `_is_categorical_col` on that subset, and takes the range from the subset too. That contradicts the new contract ("`kind_col` = the whole column — what a plot of it decides"): a subset of a fractional column can look all-integer and come out categorical. It also means a cell pop type (:501, whole-table `df`) and a track pop type of the same segmentation get different ranges, while the viewer pools both payloads [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1507 — `api/src/update_api.jl:220` — the stable-channel `api_update_check` returns early on a GitHub fetch failure. That early return has no `pendingRestart` field. `appControl.ts:168` sets `updatePending = !!d.pendingRestart`, so an offline re-check after staging clears the flag, and the "Restart to update" chip, What's New button and Settings button all disappear. The update is still staged on disk. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1507 — `api/src/update_api.jl:261` — `_check_dev` has the same early return on a GitHub fetch failure, also without `pendingRestart`. On the dev channel this makes the restart affordance vanish in the same way. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1507 — `api/test/suite/shutdown.jl:62` — the `api_app_restart` contract pin. The test asserts that the literal `_stop_children_for_exit(; stop_runner = false)` appears in the restart route body. The diff changes that call to `_stop_children_for_exit(; stop_runner)`, so `pixi run test-api` will fail. The pin needs to cover the new "default false, opt-in via `stopRunner`" form. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1507 — `app.py:228` — `_apply_pending_revert` except branch. This is the same failure shape the diff fixes in `_apply_pending_update`: when the revert fails, the `.pending-revert` marker is left on disk. `_pending_restart()` treats `.pending-revert` as "staged", so the check keeps returning `pendingRestart=true`, and the header shows "Restart to update" on every launch indefinitely. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1507 — `frontend/src/lib/whatsNew.ts:94` — `useUpdateCard` description, `updateAvailable`-only branch. The diff patched the same shape in `SettingsModule.vue:796` by adding `!appCtl.updatePending`, but missed this one. Once `pendingRestart` makes the server send `updateAvailable=false`, this card says "You're up to date (<old version>)". It sits right above the new "Restart now" button in the same What's New dialog. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1507 — `frontend/src/stores/appControl.ts:245` — `restartBackend()` (Settings → System → Restart) still POSTs `/api/app/restart` with no `stopRunner`. But the launcher applies staged updates on *every* restart, not only the new one: `app.py:261-266` calls `_apply_pending_revert()` and `_apply_pending_update()` at the top of each loop iteration. So when `pendingRestart` is true, a plain restart swaps the files in and leaves the runner on the old code. That is exactly the case the new `stopRunner` comment in `app_api.jl` says must not happen. It also waits with the short default `_waitForBackend()` and never calls `location.reload()`, while `restartToUpdate` waits 20 min (re-provisioning the env can take minutes) and then reloads. To confirm the fix route, decide whether the server should set `stop_runner` itself when `_pending_restart()` is true. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1509 — `frontend/src/modules/ViewerWindow.vue:1703` — the cell-track ribbon loop in `rebuildOverlays` still decides by `if (!pop.show || …) continue`. The capture snapshot (`ViewerWindow.vue:4635`) dropped `!p.show` and now decides by `popDrawn` only. Take a pop the manager has as `show=false` whose viewer eye has been turned on with `togglePop`. The capture now adds `(track) (vn) Tracks <path>` as visible, but the ribbon loop never draws that ribbon. The capture's legend and the screen disagree, which is the divergence `popDrawn` was meant to remove. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1510 — `api/src/viewer_api.jl:500` — `try_serve_slab`'s `preview_af` branch. The client-supplied `previewValueName` is joined straight onto `meta_dir` (`"$(af_vn)__preview_af_ch$(src_ch).ome.zarr"`). It has the same shape as the path-traversal hole this diff closes in `preview_labels_store_path` and `live_labels_store_path`, but `_label_vn_ok` is never applied here, so `previewValueName=../…` can still name a store outside the image's meta dir. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1510 — `frontend/src/modules/ChainModule.vue:1292` — `loadAllTaskDefs` / `onActivated`. The diff makes `useTaskDefs` refetch definitions whenever its page is re-activated, but this page has its own copy of the same stale-defs bug. `ChainModule` is in `KEPT_ALIVE_PAGES` (`App.vue:86`). It fetches `/api/tasks/definitions` once in `onMounted` (`:1227`), and `onActivated` only retries when `allTaskDefs` is empty (`:1293`). `selectedTaskDef` (`:1032`) builds the chain node's param form from those cached defs, so its `model` select comes from the vault list the server resolved at mount time. A model trained on Model Training after the Chain page first mounted will be missing from a node's picker until the page is reloaded. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1510 — `frontend/src/modules/ChainModule.vue:845` — `loadAllTaskDefs`: the same stale-`optionsFrom` problem this diff fixes in `useTaskDefs`. The chain node param form (`selectedTaskDef`, :1032) builds its vault `model` select from `allTaskDefs`, which are fetched once on mount. `onActivated` (:1292) only refetches when the list is empty, so a model trained, renamed or deleted after the Chains page mounted still shows the old options until the page is reloaded. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1510 — `frontend/src/modules/ViewerWindow.vue:3829` — `setBrickSource({ labelName: wantLabels ? (labelName.value || undefined) : undefined })`. This is the same vn resolver the diff switched to `maskName` in `fetchTimepoint`, `showLabels` and `wantLabels`, but this site still reads `labelName`. `brickLabelSlabUrl` (`utils/brickLoader.ts:126`) also never passes `preview`/`live`. With bricks enabled, a shown preview or live store makes `wantLabels` true, yet the bricks fetch the ticked segmentation, or nothing on a first run. So the rule that "preview/live wins over the ticked mask" does not reach the brick path. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1515 — `scripts/judge/bugs.py:476` — `sweep` carry filter, from the `fix_landed`-strip rule. The new exception keeps `fix_landed` on every carried bug with status `parked`, not only on bugs waiting for a re-check slot (those are the ones with `recheck` set). A sibling site produces `parked` bugs that still hold this pass's `fix_landed`: `scripts/judge/verify.py:248` re-parks an unparked bug as `{**b, "verify": v, "status": "parked"}`. On the next pass that stale `fix_landed` survives the carry. `_touched` (bugs.py:406) then fires on it again ("a commit naming its key landed"), so the bug is unparked and re-verified every week and holds one of the `PARKED_RECHECK` slots. `landed_counts` (record.py:234) also keeps counting it as "a fix landed" in the PR body. Before this change `fix_landed` was always stripped on carry, so this loop could not happen. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1546 — `scripts/judge/review.py:184` — `pending()` (`live()` at :189) still matches answers only on `(record["date"], event, ref)`. That is the same per-date shape `apply_reviews` just stopped using with `older=`. Suppose the owner answers a bug on an older record after the newest pass started, either with a fix session (`bug_work`) or with `open` on a decide. `applied_pass_records` now folds that answer into the newest record (it gets `fix_session` / `owner_decision`), but the bug's status stays `open`. So `work_items` still lists it, or `queue` still holds it as a decide item, and `pending` finds no row for the newest record's date or its new `B#` id. Result: `judge-review` offers the bug again. That contradicts WEEKLY_JUDGE.md: "A bug you started a fix session for isn't offered again." [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1548 — `app/test/suite/ome_qc.jl:940` — the comment "The correction-plan photon-limited card's QC signal (CORRECTION_QC_PLAN.md Q-M4)". [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1548 — `frontend/src/components/correction/CorrectionCockpit.vue:778` — a CSS comment that names `CorrectionPlanPanel` as a precedent for the `fp-body` padding convention. That component is deleted in this diff, so the comment points at a file that no longer exists. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1548 — `frontend/src/lib/iconLegend.ts:145` — the icon-legend row `pi-clipboard` → "A written plan — the correction plan". The diff deletes `CorrectionPlanPanel.vue`, the only place `pi-clipboard` meant a plan. Every remaining `pi-clipboard` use is a share/copied toast (SummaryCanvas, LayoutCanvas, ClusterPlots, GatingPlots, ViewerWindow). So the user-facing legend now describes a feature that no longer exists. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1549 — `scripts/judge/rules.py:342` — `propose`, new `answered` cut. `main()` (`pixi run judge-rules`) still calls `propose(...)` without `answered=review.rule_answers(...)`. So the printed preview counts findings from before the owner accepted or rejected a proposal, and it re-shows proposals the weekly pass (`weekly.py:361-363`) now suppresses. That contradicts what the doc table says it shows ("The rule table + proposals, printed") [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1561 — `api/src/system_api.jl:91` — `api_system_envs_install`. This sibling of `start_weights_fetch!` still uses an unconditional `start_job!(job_id)` + `Threads.@spawn _run_env_install(...)` on a fixed id, `system-env-install:<name>`. That is the check-then-start race `claim_job!` was added to close (jobs.jl docstring: "two callers racing to start the same download cannot both win"). A double-click, or two tabs, can each pass `_env_installed(name)` before either finishes. Each then spawns its own `pixi install -e cellpose-v3`, and with this diff each also spawns its own `_fetch_weights(["cyto2","cyto3"])` into the same cache. The first `finish_job!` drops the registry entry while the other run is still going, so `cancel_job!` no longer reaches its subprocesses. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1566 — `frontend/src/components/PreferencesModal.vue:307` — the Copy login command tooltip inserts the raw `roster.active`. That is the same shape this diff fixed in ProjectPanel: a bare wire profile name shown to the user without `profileNameLabel`. The button renders for every active profile, including `default`, so on a one-person install the tooltip reads "log default in" while the header and picker say `peanut`. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1568 — `api/runner.jl:17` — `service_port(:runner)`. In a standalone `pixi run runner` nothing has set `CECELIA_PORT_SLOT`, so `port_slot()` falls back to 0. The fix made `portkill.jl` and `task_console.jl` look up the user's saved slot through `current_port_slot()`; this launcher still uses only the env var. A slot-1 user who runs it on its own binds 7657, which is slot 0's runner port. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1570 — `api/src/notebooks_api.jl:104` — Pluto launch watcher (`api_notebooks_launch`'s `@async` poll): the same stop-during-warm-up shape this diff fixes for the preview worker. `_shutdown_notebook_server!` (l.263) kills `proc` and resets `_pluto_state` while this loop is still polling. The loop then sees `process_exited(proc)` and records `_pluto_state.error = "The notebook server exited during startup…"`, so a user cancel shows up as a failure. Its `finally` also clears `_pluto_state.starting` without checking the launch is still its own. Under `api_notebooks_restart` (shutdown → relaunch), the old watcher can write the error onto the new launch and clear the new launch's `starting`. That is the side-car-flag clobbering this diff removes from `_preview_starting`. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1570 — `app/src/preview.jl:176` — `launch!` readiness loop. It only checks `protocol == PREVIEW_PROTOCOL` before accepting whoever answers on :7656. This is the adoption check the hunk fixed in `_ensure_preview!` (protocol only, env never compared). On a `:switch`, `_free_preview_port!` waits at most 5 s (20 × 0.25 s). If the old protocol-18 worker from the other env is still answering after that, `launch!` logs "connected" and returns `w` with `w.env` set to the new env while the process serving requests runs the old one. So a cellpose 3 preview can still reach cellpose 4. The ping reply already carries `env`, so the check is available here. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1570 — `app/src/preview.jl:178` — `launch!`'s readiness ping. It has the same check as the adoption guard this diff fixed: the old guard trusted a worker on the protocol number alone. `_ensure_preview!`'s adoption now also requires `env` to match, but `launch!` still returns "connected" for any reply with `protocol == PREVIEW_PROTOCOL`. On a `:switch`, `_free_preview_port!` waits at most 5 s (20 × 0.25 s). If the old worker in the other env is still answering after that, it speaks protocol 18 as well. Its ping then satisfies the new launch, and `_preview_ref[]` records `env = :cellpose_v3` while the process actually serving is the default-env one. That reproduces #1555. The code comment already admits that a kill is asynchronous and the old process can answer the readiness ping. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1570 — `app/src/preview.jl:203` — `launch!` readiness guard. It accepts the first ping that answers with `protocol == PREVIEW_PROTOCOL` and never compares the reply's `env` to `w.env`. The diff added the env check to the adoption probe in `_ensure_preview!` but not to this sibling guard. On a `:switch`, `_free_preview_port!` waits at most 20×0.25 s = 5 s. If the old worker is still answering after that (it speaks protocol 18 too, just in the other env), the new launch takes its ping as "connected" and returns `w` with `w.env = :cellpose_v3`, while the process actually serving is still cellpose 4. That is the #1555 shape on the switch path. Before this diff the protocol bump made a stale worker's ping fail this check; a same-protocol worker in the other env now passes it [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1570 — `app/src/runner/client.jl:167` — `runner_launch!` polls `process_running(h.proc)` on the shared handle field, and `runner_stop!` (client.jl:193) sets `h.proc = nothing` from another task. This is the bug the hunk fixed in `launch!` (`process_running(::Nothing)` MethodError while a stop lands mid-warm-up). It is reachable: `api_runner_restart` (api/src/runner_api.jl:357) runs the launch with `Threads.@spawn`, which can wait up to 120 s. During that wait, Quit (app_api.jl:62) or a second restart (runner_api.jl:352) calls `runner_stop!`, and the error gets logged as "Task runner failed to relaunch". The handle has no state or cancel signal, and it does not poll a local `proc`. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1574 — `mcp/tests/test_discovery.py:131` — `ServerToggleTest._server_view` starts a child interpreter with `env={**os.environ, "PYTHONPATH": str(MCP_DIR)}`. That replaces the `$PIXI_PROJECT_ROOT/python` pin which `pixi.toml:253-255` now relies on for `test-mcp`. The child runs `from cecelia_mcp import server`, which imports `client` and then `cecelia.utils.loopback`, so it hits the same unpinned-`python/` failure. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1574 — `scripts/agent_eval/board_render.py:207` — `render_boards` → `connect(ws_url, …, additional_headers=auth_headers())`. The token is sent to Chromium's DevTools socket (`ws_url` is the `DevTools listening on ws://…` URL from `_launch`), not to Cecelia. The page's real `/api/*` calls go through `_Cdp._serve`, which calls `Fetch.continueRequest` with no `headers` override and has no sign-in cookie. Those calls now get 401, and `Page.navigate` to `{api}/` gets the sign-in HTML instead of the app. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1574 — `scripts/agent_eval/run_app.py:67` — `mcp_config` builds the env for both `cecelia_mcp.server` and `cecelia_mcp.autonomous_server` with `PYTHONPATH = REPO/mcp` only. This is the same shape the diff fixes in `observer_mcp_spec` / `_observer_pythonpath` (`app/src/ai/agent_runner.jl:274`). Both servers now import `cecelia.utils.loopback` (through `client.py`, `wsclient.py` and `autonomous.py`), and this process isn't launched by `pixi run`, so `[activation.env]` never pins `python/`. `cecelia` then resolves through the env's editable install, which may point at a checkout that doesn't have `utils/loopback.py`. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1576 — `app/src/tasks/importImages/omezarr/task.jl:119` — the caller of `bf2raw_chunk_flags` still branches on `isempty(chunk_flags)` and logs "auto (1024, capped to the frame)". The changed contract never returns empty, so that branch is dead and its text describes the old behaviour. The log line itself prints correctly ("512"), but the branch encodes the removed empty-for-auto contract. The comment at :114–117 also still says "bioformats2raw's 1024". [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1576 — `app/src/tasks/importImages/peek_pyramid_run.py:52` — `DEFAULT_CHUNK = 1024` (static-image target used by `target_for_shape`/`recommend_levels`). The docstring at :20 says it "matches bioformats2raw's XY chunk", and the `bf2raw_chunk_flags` hunk changes that chunk to 512. Example: a static 2048² image gets 2 recommended levels, so the deepest level is 1024² with 512² chunks, a 2×2 grid. `qc.jl::pyramid_layout` reads the store's real chunk (512), so `pyramid_qc_findings` will flag `import.pyramid_too_shallow` on a fresh import that used the recommended defaults. The "agrees with `qc.jl::pyramid_layout`" claim at :23 and :65 no longer holds. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1583 — `frontend/src/lib/webgpu/brickVolumeRenderer.ts:735` — `buildAtlas(n)` → `labelTextures = layouts.map(...)`. The fix limits the intensity atlases to `layouts.slice(0, n)` for `createBrickAtlasTextures` and the returned `layouts`. This label-atlas copy in the same function still maps over the full `layouts`. When labels are on and an OOM steps down to n < layouts.length, it allocates layouts.length label textures, which adds the VRAM the step-down was meant to free. `labAtlasEntries` then indexes `vb.labAtlas[i]!` past its length of n (`brickShader.ts:129`, length exactly n), so the bind group gets `binding: undefined`. That is a validation error the 'out-of-memory' scope does not catch. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1583 — `frontend/src/modules/ViewerWindow.vue:1406` — `estimateAtlasesForBudget` is a mirror of `pickAtlasLayout` that still uses the old `floor(budgetMB / perAtlasMB)` count. The fix at `brickAtlas.ts:179-181` changes the picker to `ceil` and an even split, so a 6 GB budget now builds 2 atlases. The chip suffix (`:1415`) and `effectiveCacheMBLabel` (`:1441`) still say 1. Its doc comment (`:1400-1405`) also still says "floor(budget / one atlas)… never allocates a partial atlas". [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1583 — `frontend/src/modules/ViewerWindow.vue:2248` — the flat-path `pump` and `schedulePump` both compute `Math.sign(tp - lastT) || 1`. This is the same expression on the same `lastT` that the `gotoT` hunk (`:2328`) fixed because it read the loop wrap (last → 0) as reverse play. At the wrap, `prefetchWindow(0, -1, …)` throws away its ahead slots. `schedulePump`'s `keep` set then aborts in-flight fetches for t=1..k, which forward play needs next. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1584 — `docs/MAINTAINABILITY.md:185` — a second copy of the same old rule text. It still calls `open_as_zarr(as_dask=True)` the sanctioned entry and says "library utils under `python/cecelia/**` are unrestricted". The new `CLAUDE.md` rule and `test_dask_import_isolation.py` now ban dask imports at load time under `python/cecelia/utils/`, so this is a direct contradiction. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1584 — `docs/MODULES.md:415` — the zarr-conventions example a new task author copies opens input with `open_as_zarr(params['imPath'], as_dask=True)`. That is the pattern this diff flips to plain zarr in both runners and in the preview worker. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1584 — `preview/preview_worker.py:300` — `PreviewState.image` still opens dask levels with `open_as_zarr(..., as_dask=True)`. Its docstring (296–298) gives the reason: `channel_histograms` needs a lazy handle to stream. The diff removes that reason. `channel_histograms` now reads one plane at a time with `np.asarray(arr[sl])`. These dask levels still reach it through `norm_params` (865) → `seg._compute_norm_params` (349) → `channel_histograms(level, ...)` (segmentation_utils.py:826). On a dask array, every plane read rebuilds and executes a graph. That is the access pattern `image_zarr`'s docstring measured at 9.3× slower (310–313) and `crop_at_t` measured at 12× (610–616). The diff already moved two siblings off the dask open (saturation_run.py, register_run.py), but it does not move this one. It is also the last dask open in a shipped Python process, so the resident preview worker still imports dask. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1584 — `python/cecelia/tests/test_zarr_access_convention.py:18` — the old "`dask.array` is opt-in" contract. The module docstring and the assertion messages still tell an offending runner that `zarr_utils.open_as_zarr(..., as_dask=True)` is "the sanctioned entry". The diff removes that wording from `CLAUDE.md` and moves `saturation_run`/`register_run` off `as_dask=True`, so these lines now point people at a path the new rule no longer endorses. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1586 — `docs/todo/spike/webgpu/slab_bench.jl:66` — `slab_bytes` was renamed to `with_slab_bytes(f, vol)` and its signature changed, but the bench still calls `slab_bytes(vol)` at lines 66, 67 and 68. It loads the reader half of `viewer_api.jl` through `include_string`, so it now hits an `UndefVarError` the first time it calls it. The comment at line 28 also still names `slab_bytes`. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1592 — `docs/inventory/JULIA_APP.md:73` — the `tls_desired` / `set_tls_desired!` entry still lists the old signature, `tls_desired(; is_dev)`, and the old default, "on in prod, off in dev". The diff dropped the `is_dev` keyword and made the default off everywhere. Anyone who copies the signature from the inventory gets a keyword-argument error. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1592 — `docs/todo/spike/webgpu/slab_cache_bench.sh:16` — the header's Env list still says `HTTP=2 (default) or 1.1`. This hunk updates the comment on line 13 to match the new plain-HTTP/1.1 default, but line 16 still contradicts line 30 (`HTTP=${HTTP:-1.1}  # 1.1 (the server's default)`) and line 9 (`HTTP/1.1 by default, like the server`). It is the same stale "HTTP/2 + TLS is the default" wording, left unfixed. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1592 — `scripts/agent_eval/board_render.py:215` — the comment that sets up the scheme probe still says "installed apps: HTTPS, self-signed". This is the same claim the diff corrected in `app.py`, `loopback.py` and `cecelia-connect.sh`. The code is fine because it probes and resolves the scheme. Only the comment is wrong. It is a code comment, not a doc. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1606 — `frontend/src/lib/webgpu/brickVolumeRenderer.ts:875` — `kickFetch`'s `inflight.delete(key)` in `.then`/`.catch`. This is the same shape as the `fetchTimepoint` `.finally` fix. The delete is unconditional and runs after `setImage`/`setZLo`/`setSource` have done `inflight.forEach(abort); inflight.clear()` (`:627`, `:1878`, `:1988`). A new fetch for the same brick key may already have put its own `AbortController` in the map. When the old aborted fetch's `.catch` fires, it deletes that new entry. After that, the next clear can't abort the new request, and `inflight.has(key)` (`:860`) returns false, so the same brick gets fetched twice. Fix pattern is in the diff: delete only if `inflight.get(key) === ac`. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1606 — `frontend/src/lib/webgpu/tileRenderer.ts:474` — `uploadTile` registers the tile with `tiles.set(...)` after `await device.queue.onSubmittedWorkDone()` and checks only `usable()`. It never checks `allocGen`, which is the check this diff adds to the flat renderer's `uploadFrame`. If `setImage` → `dropAtlas` (`:283`, which bumps `allocGen` and clears `tiles`/`freeSlots`) runs during that await, the bytes went to the destroyed atlas. The tile is still recorded in the new one: its slot has no data and is also in the new `freeSlots`. `hasTile` then returns true, so the pump never refetches it. The `await pendingAlloc` at `:420` has the same gap. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1606 — `frontend/src/modules/ViewerWindow.vue:2519` — `fetchTile`'s `finally` runs `tileAborts.delete(kStr)` with no ownership check. This is the same shape as the `fetchTimepoint` finally this diff fixes. `changeVersion` (`:4565-4566`) aborts and then clears `tileAborts`. If the tile pump re-requests the same `kStr` for the new version, the old fetch's `finally` (which runs when its abort lands) deletes the new fetch's controller. The next version switch then can't abort that fetch, and the `existing` dedup guard at `:2443` stops seeing it, so the same tile can be fetched twice at once. [**fixed_pre_commit**]

- #1625 — `app/src/tasks/scheduler/jobs.jl:144` — → `close_run_log!(tgt, job.id, …)` (`app/src/run_log.jl:150`). The new guard lets a restart register while the cancelled old run is still winding down. If the old run closes its log after the restart has opened its own entry, it stamps `cancelled` on the new run's entry, and its own entry stays `running` until it is reaped as `interrupted`. - #1676 — `scripts/judge/auto_fix.py:464` — `_log` rewrites a stopped fixed bug's reason as `"Stopped before the PR: {stopped}. …"`. The contract changed in this diff: `stop()` (`:634-636`) now also logs stops that happen after the PR opened. These cover red CI after `CI_ROUNDS`, a conflict, a CI timeout and a failed follow-up push. Each is logged with `pr_url` set and `stage="pr"`, but still gets the "before the PR" prefix. That reason feeds the `[f]` brief (`review._staged_line`: "What it said: …") and the card's Auto-fix section (`review.py:600`). As a result they say "Stopped before the PR" right next to "opened its PR … Its PR: <url>". The `_log` docstring (`:456`, "stopped before the PR") and the `STAGES` comment (`:77`, "How far a session stopped before its PR got") describe the same old assumption [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1676 — `scripts/judge/auto_fix.py:737` — the `stop(..., stage="pr")` calls that don't name the PR. The new `review._STAGED["pr"]` (review.py:324) promises the `[f]` brief that "the PR is named below". The only place the brief can show it is the row's `reason` (`_staged_line`, review.py:331), because `_log` writes `pr_url: ""` for any stopped row. The CI-red, timeout and "still fails after rounds" stops put `{pr}` in their reason. These two don't: "merging main conflicts in …" and "resolving the conflict with main: …". So `[f]` is told a PR is named and gets none. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1676 — `scripts/judge/auto_fix.py:751` — (`session(conflict_brief(files), …)`) and **scripts/judge/auto_fix.py:766** (`session(ci_brief(...), …)`): `default_session` raises `RateLimited` when the reset is more than `MAX_WAIT` away. The recital-round session already handles that (`except RateLimited as e: stop(...); raise` at `auto_fix.py:677`, added in 388ce8dd, "a usage limit no longer strands an --auto bundle"). These two new follow-up sessions in the CI/conflict loop have no such guard, so the exception skips `stop()`. No `auto_fix.jsonl` row gets written with `tree` or `stage="pr"`, and the worktree is left behind (in the conflict path, with the merge still in progress). On the next run `work_one` finds that `tree.exists()` and skips the bundle, but `auto_items` still treats it as eligible because there is no `left` row. That is the stranded bundle 388ce8dd fixed for the recital round. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1676 — `scripts/judge/issues.py:211` — `title()` uses the same `f"{b['file']}:{b['line']}" if b.get("file")` pattern. A filed bug with no location therefore gets ``**Where:** [`?:0`](https://github.com/<repo>/blob/<sha>/?#L0)``, a broken GitHub link, instead of the `file:line` that verify's evidence names. `filed()` (`issues.py:200-204`) files exactly the `verdict: fix` case the new test uses, so this path is reachable. - #1786 — `frontend/src/components/HpcSettings.vue:106` — `install()`, the same race the diff fixes in `runTest`. `runTest` now waits for `pendingSave` before it calls the backend, but `install()` calls `installHpcEnv()` straight away. - #1793 — `app/src/tasks/spatialAnalysis/cellContacts.jl:159` — `label_props(props_path) |> add_obs(out) |> save!`, the points route's writer for the same `{pt}.cell.*#{target}` column set. The points route writes `contact#` / `min_distance#` / `contact_id#` for the same target and doesn't drop those two columns. - #1796 — `frontend/src/utils/hpcRun.ts:140` — `hpcRunEnvNote` is a second reader of `HpcEnvStatus`. The diff gave `hpcEnvLine` (hpcSettings.ts) a new `e.cleaning` case, but this sibling has none. - #1800 — `frontend/src/modules/TasksModule.vue:485` — `CcProgressBar v-if="selected.status === 'running' && …"`. - #1806 — `scripts/judge/issues.py:282` — `_closing` checks the raw `v.get("verdict")` against `"dismiss"` / `("dismiss", "not_a_bug")` and doesn't go through `record.verify_verdict`, which the hunk adds. Verify's new `fixed` verdict on a repeated agent error sets status `dismissed` with verdict `"fixed"`. Neither branch matches that, so it falls through to "Dismissed by the judge", closed as not planned. That names the wrong source and gives the wrong reason for a verify-confirmed fix. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1809 — `scripts/judge/auto_fix.py:756` — `ERROR_MARKER in recital`. This is the same whole-body substring read that the hunk at :763 just narrowed. A reviewer finding that quotes the literal `RECITAL SCRIPT ERROR` (likely when the diff touches this code or `recital.py:338`) makes a clean recital look errored. `reviewer_errors(recital)` then returns `[]`, `rate_limit` gets an empty string, and the session stops with "recital's reviewers errored" even though both reviewers ran. Using `reviewer_errors(recital)` as the test would match the narrowed read. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1816 — `frontend/src/modules/TasksModule.vue:362` — `actions-width="2.9rem"` on the TasksModule SelectionTable. It has the same undersized-actions-column problem this hunk fixes in TaskList. The table sets `column-width-key`, which makes it resizable and therefore `sized`. A sized table gets `overflow: hidden` and ellipsis on its cells, so the column clips the buttons the same way [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1818 — `frontend/src/modules/ViewerWindow.vue:1905` — `reconcilePopEyes` one-at-a-time branch (`soloOn`). It is called once per segmentation (`for (const vn of …)`), so `soloOn` only turns off pops in that segmentation's `next`. One-at-a-time mode covers every segmentation, though: `soloPopEyes(popEyes.value, listedPops.value, …)` at :1943 works across all segmentations, and `listedPops` spans every `vn`. With two segmentations listed, switching a pop on in the manager for segmentation A leaves the pop that was on in segmentation B still on, so two pops show in one-at-a-time mode. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1836 — `mcp/cecelia_mcp/server.py:591` — and **:599**: `gate_plot` / `gate_cells_view`. The tool-scraping regex at `api/test/suite/kiwi_turn.jl:41` still allows only whitespace between the registrar line and `def`. These two tools put a second decorator, `@with_doc(...)`, in that gap (`@_tool` → `@with_doc(GATE_*_DOC)` → `def`), so they never land in `tools`. They are also missing from `KIWI_READ_TOOLS` and `KIWI_EXCLUDED_TOOLS`, so the set-equality test at :43 passes while neither tool has been decided. The guarantee "every observer tool is decided … a new tool fails here" is broken for any registrar plus a stacked decorator, which is the same shape as the #1476 entry in `docs/ai-assist/EFFECTIVENESS.md`. Today it hits `@_tool`; the new `_gui_tool` branch would hit it the same way. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1841 — `docs/API.md:85` — the `POST /api/viewer/capture` row still says "(additive, no delete, no mutate)". The same file lists the delete route at `docs/API.md:206`, so the doc contradicts itself [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1841 — `docs/inventory/MCP.md:32` — the share-in captures entry still says "Additive-only, no delete/mutate". That is the same stale claim the diff corrects in `api/src/captures_api.jl:9`. - #1841 — `frontend/src/lib/tips.ts:188` — `TIPS` entry `tip-claude-mcp`, step `'Run claude in a terminal.'`. A plain `claude` in a terminal skips the session that Start in terminal builds, which is already pointed at this project. Its other steps (`Kiwi → Assistant → Set up (once).`, `Ask: "check my current project in cecelia"`) match the old `Chat to Claude` steps, so the daily tip and the Claude overview now give different setup instructions. - #1847 — `api/src/runner_api.jl:61` — the runner relay's outcome bank (`record_task_outcome!` on `chain:node:done`/`failed`). In-process, `chain_frames.jl` `bank` now skips any frame with `waited`, and the new `waiting` handler banks prepare. The relay copy got neither change. The frame carries no `waited` flag, so a runner-hosted manual step continued by the user gets banked again with blank `startedAt`/`finishedAt`. `record_task_outcome!` then fills in now and a lost start: the restamp the in-process guard exists to prevent. A `chain:node:waiting` frame is also never banked here, so prepare's outcome never reaches `/api/tasks/recent` in runner mode. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1847 — `app/src/gating/popmanager/picker.jl:195` — `ensure_filter_pop!`. It loads the gating map, changes it and saves it again (`load_pop_map` → `add_pop!`/`del_pop!` → `save_pop_map!`) without taking `with_popmap_lock`. That is the case this diff moves the lock into the package to cover: "a task that writes gating serialises with these handlers". `tasks/spatialAnalysis/detectAggregates.jl:106` and `aggregatesMeshes.jl:61` call it with `flow`/`track` maps, the same sidecars the API handlers and `copy_gating!` now guard. If one of those tasks runs in the server process while the user edits gates on the same image, one write can overwrite the other. `copy.jl:8-10` and the new `docs/inventory/JULIA_APP.md` line both say every gating load→save holds the lock, and this one doesn't. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1847 — `frontend/src/components/canvas/CanvasArrangeButtons.vue:75` — the generic `.cc-btn-group > .cc-btn` rule and the `.cc-btn-group > .cc-btn + .cc-btn` divider rule in `style.css:379,383`. These have the same direct-child shape that was just fixed for `button.cc-del`. Here, a `<ConfirmButton>` wraps a `button.cc-btn` (Close all) inside a `.cc-btn-group`. The button ends up inside `span.confirm-btn` (`display: contents`), so it is never the group's direct child. That means the group's `border-radius: 0` / `background: var(--cc-surface-2)` and the left divider after the Cascade button never apply to it. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1847 — `mcp/cecelia_mcp/autonomous.py:403` — `chain_state` decides `active` from the node statuses `("queued","running")`. Plan A8 says `waiting` must be reported as settled-not-finished. [**fixed_pre_commit**]

- #1866 — `api/src/gating_api.jl:1173` — `api_gating_summary` builds `xt`/`yt` with bare `_axis_transform(q, …)` and does not call `_auto_params`. Its own docstring says it reads "the SAME read as the gate plot… in the coordinates a gate is written in". MCP `gate_histogram` and `gate_plot` send the same query (`gv.plot_query` → `axis_transform_query`, `mcp/cecelia_mcp/autonomous.py:441`). So for an unparameterised `{"kind":"logicle"}` or `{"kind":"asinh"}`, the picture route (`gating_views_api.jl:39`) now uses the channel-derived T/cofactor while the histogram's quantiles stay at T=262144 / cof=150. So an agent moving a gate it drew with a bare `{"kind":"logicle"}` gets it re-stored in a different transform from the one `gate_plot`/`gate_histogram` showed it [**fixed_pre_commit**]

But `onParentUp` returns early when `props.mode !== 'off'`, so nothing can be selected while a draw tool is armed. - #1872 — `frontend/src/utils/movies.ts:62` — `movieDisplayName`. - #1876 — `app/src/storage.jl:614` — `_rename_ccid_fields!`, the flat `meta.funParams[fun]` blob (`FUN_PARAMS_META_KEY`, written on every run by `write_module_fun_params!`, `app/src/model/image.jl:683-686`). The rename only rewrites `funParamsByName`. `read_module_fun_params` falls back to the flat blob for a name with nothing banked (`image.jl:621-628`), so the form gets pre-filled with a population on a segmentation that no longer exists. - #1886 — `scripts/judge/auto_fix.py:609` — `followup_message`, the commit message for a CI or conflict follow-up round inside the shared `_work` (called at `:955`). - #1887 — `docs/API.md:162` — the `/api/figures/delete-own` row still says it deletes "(file, side files, registry entry)". The hunk updated the `/api/figures/render` row above it to "the only file kept" but left this one. [**fixed_pre_commit**]

- #1901 — `app/src/tasks/clustTracks/cluster.jl:98` — `_run_task(::ClustTracks)` pools the selected `clusterMeasures` across images by column name. This is the same index-means-marker problem `clustPops` now refuses (cluster.jl:157), but this sibling still pools images whose channel order differs [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1901 — `frontend/src/modules/gate/GatingCopyDialog.vue:86` — `canvas.copyEntry` (`stores/canvasPanels.ts:40`) is the other half of the same copy. It deep-copies the source's gate-plot panels as they are, and each panel's axes are stored as raw index columns (`props.ui.x`/`ui.y` = `mean_intensity_N`, `GatePlotPanel.vue:73`). `copy_gating!` now gives a target with a different channel order (anything in `r.remapped`) its own gate columns, but the copied layout still points at the source's indices. This diff stops drawing the running week, and the footer legend at `chart.py:661` now says "only partly logged", so this text is out of date. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1906 — `app/src/qc.jl:275` — `metadata_qc_findings` Z-ratio check. This is the same bug the `_resample_z_inherited_meta` hunk fixes: it computes `phys_z / phys_x` from the raw stored values and never converts each axis by its own unit (`PhysicalSizeZUnit` against `PhysicalSizeUnit`). An image with X in nm and Z in µm gets a ratio off by 1000×, so it raises a false `metadata.z_spacing_unusual` (or hides a real one). It also checks only `PhysicalSizeUnit` and ignores the per-axis unit key that `_meta_axis_length_unit` now honours. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1906 — `app/src/tasks/editImages/resampleZ.jl:33` — `_resample_z_inherited_meta` is the Julia twin of the resampleZ runner hunk. It still computes `ratio = px_z / px_x` from the raw `PhysicalSizeX`/`PhysicalSizeZ` without converting by unit (`_meta_axis_length_unit` / `length_unit_um`). It also writes `out["PhysicalSizeZ"] = px_x`, and it copies only `PhysicalSizeUnit`, not `PhysicalSizeZUnit`. So when the axes have different units (x in nm, z in µm), the `SizeZ` it registers in ccid no longer matches the plane count the Python runner now writes. This is the same unit-blind ratio the hunk fixed. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1906 — `frontend/src/tasks/ParamRenderer.vue:550` — `groupPxSize` is documented as "µm per pixel for the strip's pixel captions". Two images with the same physical calibration but different stored units (0.5 µm vs 500 nm) also fail the "every image agrees" check. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1906 — `frontend/src/tasks/paramAdvisors.ts:936` — the `anisotropyBoxUm` advisor passes the raw `img.physicalSizeX` to `frameGeometry(…, umPerPx)`. That value is then used as µm/px: `boxUm / umPerPx` at :203–204, and the "calibrated at … µm/px" tip at :239. It has the same shape as the `ViewerPanel.vue` scale-bar fix, which now wraps the value in `pixelSizeUm(…, physicalSizeUnit)`. On an image stored in nm the box estimate comes out 1000× off. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1906 — `python/cecelia/utils/correction_utils.py:1867` — `af_mask_sigmas` divides `xy_um`/`z_um` by the raw `im_physical_size`, which has the same "value is µm" assumption [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1906 — `python/cecelia/utils/label_import_utils.py:382` — `object_stats(frame_sizes, (im_physical_size('x'), …('y'), …('z')))` on label import. These are the same raw reads the fix replaced in segmentation and feed the same µm-denominated `seg.*` findings, so an image calibrated in nm or mm gets object sizes in the wrong unit. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1906 — `python/cecelia/utils/segmentation_utils.py:171` — `phys_size_x/y` from `dim_utils.im_physical_size`, the raw OME value with no unit conversion. It turns the cell parameters given in µm into pixels, so the same nm/mm-as-µm assumption the hunk removes from `img_physical_sizes` is still here. A pixel size in nm gives a diameter in px that is 1000× off [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1906 — `python/cecelia/utils/segmentation_utils.py:824` — `object_stats` gets a scale tuple of `(phys_size_x, phys_size_y, im_physical_size('z'))`. The hunk at :171 moved x/y to `im_physical_size_um`, but z still uses the raw `im_physical_size`. On an nm-calibrated stack the tuple mixes µm (x/y) with nm (z), so the 3-D `seg.*` object sizes come out 1000× off along z. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1911 — `app/src/tasks/segment/measure_labels.jl:100` — `prune_stale_clustfeatures!` exists so a run's sidecar entry is dropped when its table is rebuilt. The diff makes `_hmm_delete!` (`app/src/analysis_runs.jl:203`) drop the `hmmfit` key along with the state column, but there is no prune for `hmmfit`. When the table is rebuilt (`measure_labels`, outside the carry-over path), the `live.cell.hmm.state.*` columns are lost but `{vn}.hmmfit.json` keeps its entries. `hmm_fit_runs` (`app/src/plotting/hmm_fit_curve.jl`) reads every sidecar entry without checking that the column still exists, so the elbow keeps plotting those runs. The runs list builds from obs columns (`analysis_runs.jl:190`), so these runs can't be deleted from the UI either [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1921 — `scripts/judge/chart.py:211` — `_REVIEWER_OF[e["event"]]`. The map only has the fanout and convention finding events, but it is applied to every row in `FINDING_EVENTS`, which comes from `log.py:268`. This diff adds `result_review_finding` to that set. The first `result_review_finding` row then raises `KeyError`. The plan says the four backtest rows are already logged, so this will happen. The weekly README chart then fails every pass. `chart.py:804` catches the error, so the judge itself keeps running. `_RUN_OF` and `REVIEWERS` at :102–104 need the same third key. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1923 — `scripts/judge/bugs.py:776` — the sweep's verdict→status mapping (`_VERDICT_STATUS`, `not_a_bug → "dismissed"`). The new `shipped_reproduced` guard at verify.py:281 stops an agent from closing a `reproduced` result finding that its author tagged as not fixed. It only applies inside `verify()`, but the sweep's excerpt judge runs first and can dismiss the same finding. If it answers `not_a_bug`, the bug's status becomes `dismissed`. `verify.eligible` (verify.py:147) only accepts `status == "open"`, so the finding never reaches the guard and never goes to the owner as `decide`. The only escape hatch, `_verified_live(b)` at bugs.py:770, applies only after an earlier verify, which a new result finding hasn't had. This is the "session can't tag a failing repro away" guarantee, with an agent closing the finding on a second path. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1924 — `README.md:263` — the alt text for effectiveness.svg still describes panel b as "for each of the two reviewers (fan-out audit and convention check)". `chart.py` `REVIEWERS` now draws three series (result review added), and the same diff updates the panel f wording and the footer, but not this line. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1924 — `docs/ai-assist/AGENT_RECORD.md:9` — still says "read by two reviewer agents (`pixi run recital`)". The same diff changes "the two reviewers'" to "the reviewers'" at line 43 of this file for this reason, and leaves line 9 out. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1939 — `app/src/tasks/behaviour/hmm_transitions.jl:91` — `write_qc(img, "behaviour.hmm_transitions", string(vn), …)`. This is the same bug the hunk fixes in `hmm_states.jl:138`. Each run of the `behaviour.hmm` composite (states → transitions) writes a new `trans.<colName>` column, so a K = 2…5 sweep overwrites the previous run's transitions QC. `cohort_runs` also returns `[]` for this fun, so the Check-cohort button has no run selector for the transitions stage (`cohortStages.ts:13` lists both HMM funs) [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1939 — `frontend/src/components/CohortCheckButton.vue:29` — comment on the run-selector contract still says "Funs that keep no runs (segment/tracking/HMM) return []". With this change the HMM page's cohort button will show a run selector. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1944 — `api/src/routes/lab_log.jl:142` — `api_images_meta_resync` comment lists the current-probe fields as "per-plane profile, range, noise" for the `measure: true` re-probe. That is the same `SATURATION_CURRENT_KEYS` set, now widened to include `floor`. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1944 — `app/src/tasks/importImages/omezarr/ccid_sync.jl:155` — `resync_ome_meta!` acquisition guard. The new contract is that a missing `acquisition` comes with an `acquisitionMissing` reason, and that is only written inside `_recover_acquisition_settings!`. This caller skips the call in two cases: when `register_reference_uid` is set, and when `ori_path` is empty (line 157). Neither case records a reason. The payload then sends `acquisitionMissing = nothing`, and `acquisitionNotice` falls through to "Not read yet: refresh metadata". Refreshing can never change that message for these images. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1944 — `app/src/tasks/importImages/omezarr/saturation.jl:52` — the `ensure_saturation_meta!` docstring. It is a hand-copied list of the same constant and now leaves out `floor`. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1944 — `frontend/src/components/ImageMetadataDialog.vue:52` — this comment describes the `ensure_saturation_meta!` re-probe trigger as "per-plane profile, range, noise floor". It is a third description of the same key list as the two comments this diff fixed (`api/src/routes/lab_log.jl:142`, `saturation.jl:52`), and it still leaves out `floor`, which is now in `SATURATION_CURRENT_KEYS`. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1947 — `scripts/judge/auto_fix.py:1098` — `run`: `colour, width = out.isatty(), _width()` is read once at the start of the run and reused for every bundle's `_hr` header, `_rv.describe` and `outcome_rows`. This is the same "tty read at start" shape the hunk fixed in `Progress.__exit__` and `_spin`. If the terminal goes after the first session, every later header still gets ANSI codes, now written into the `FallbackOut` log file. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1947 — `scripts/judge/deps_fix.py:219` — `run`: `colour = out.isatty()` is read once before the PR loop and used for each PR's `_hr` header and the closing summary. `judge-deps` now calls `_af.unattended(name="deps")`, so the terminal can go mid-loop, and later lines then carry escape codes into `deps-<ts>-terminal-lost.log`. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1949 — `.claude/hooks/check_commit_recital.py:441` — `agent_recital_gate`, the widened `RESULT_NOT_RUN in message` guard. It is a bare substring test over the whole message. The other matchers that read recital text are built so that quoted text doesn't trip them. The hook's own marker count at `check_commit_recital.py:283` runs through `outside_code` first, and `reviewer_errors`/`_ERROR_BLOCK` (`recital.py:65,77`) anchor on the `> **RECITAL SCRIPT ERROR** —` block shape. That docstring says why: findings "may quote anything". This guard does neither, so any finding that quotes the tail blocks the commit. The already-reported finding [fanout-9079fc00] quotes `` `_Result review: not run — no git dir to key the review on_` `` word for word. A delta round now re-prints that finding at the top of the section ("earlier rounds on this change"), so this commit's own message contains the substring and the gate blocks it, even though the result review ran. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1952 — `app/src/tasks/exportImages/ome_tiff.jl:44` — `_export_calibration` → `ome_xml_unit_name`. This is the pass-through shape the hunk fixed in `sync_zarr_calibration!`. A unit missing from `_OME_XML_UNIT` is written as-is into `PhysicalSize*Unit` / `TimeIncrementUnit`. The site's own comment says such a value makes `<Pixels>` schema-invalid, and Bio-Formats then discards the whole OME block. The sync path now goes through `_ome_unit_symbol` and leaves the value out. The export path still uses the bare `ome_xml_unit_name`. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1952 — `app/src/tasks/importImages/omezarr/task.jl:270` — the comment on the `sync_zarr_calibration!(resolved_zarr, …)` call says the OME-XML "can't reach it via this path (update_ome_xml_pixels walks `<resolved>/OME/…`)". That describes the exact bug this diff fixes. After the change the call reaches the root OME-XML, so the comment now documents the opposite of what the code does. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1952 — `app/test/suite/task_validation.jl:265` — OME unit-writer ratchet (`ome_xml_unit_name` presence check), stale contract. The fix moves every OME-XML unit writer (`sync_zarr_calibration!`, `_export_calibration`) from `ome_xml_unit_name` to `_ome_unit_symbol`. The ratchet still only checks that the old converter's name appears somewhere in the file or its directory. `ome_tiff.jl` now passes only because a comment (line 36) mentions `ome_xml_unit_name`, and `calibration.jl` passes because it defines it. A new writer that calls `ome_xml_unit_name` would satisfy the ratchet, yet that function still copies an unknown unit through as written (the `"furlong"` case this fix removes), so the guard approves exactly the shape the fix exists to stop [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1952 — `python/cecelia/utils/zarr_utils.py:1346` — `write_calibration` OME-XML half, the Python twin of `sync_zarr_calibration!`. It has the same unknown-unit shape the Julia hunk fixed. `_OME_XML_UNIT.get(unit_for_axis['T'], 's')` falls back to `'s'` for any unmapped time unit and still writes `time_increment`. A `µs`/`ns`/`d` interval from `dim_utils` would be stamped as that number of seconds. The Julia side now leaves the value out instead. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1956 — `app/src/qc.jl:185` — `_SATURATION_WARN_SIGNAL_FRAC` header comment, same contract stated in a second place: it still calls this the "Level at which clipping raises a FINDING" that should "stay silent otherwise". After this change it is only the `warn`/`info` boundary, and anything detected below it is an `info` finding. The docstring was updated but this comment was not. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1966 — `docs/OBJECTMODEL.md:199` — the `acquisition` field description still ends "read by `_recover_acquisition_settings!` (… → `read_acquisition_settings_run.py` → `oir_meta.py`) … `.oir` only so far." This change adds `.czi` to `ACQUISITION_SETTINGS_FORMATS` and `_READERS`, so "`.oir` only" no longer holds. It is a hand-restated copy of the format gate that was not updated. I read it, it matches, and it needs a fix. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1966 — `docs/todo/ACQUISITION_ADVISOR_PLAN.md:405` — the new plan note says "the dialog row still says Bright Z". The same change adds `depthRampName(acq)` and uses it for both the `acquisitionRows` key and the `lightPathStages` power items. So a `.czi` dialog row now reads "Auto Z brightness", and the frontend test `acquisitionSettings.test.ts:105` pins that. The plan now states the opposite of the code it describes. I read the line and it needs a fix. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1972 — `docs/todo/ACQUISITION_CARD_PLAN.md:113` — D6 ("One merged cell for the detectors: 'Auto HV on — file records HV <n> · gain · offset'") and card layout §3 ("merged HV/gain/offset cell (D6)") still describe the merged cell. This change split that cell at `acquisitionCard.ts:89-96` and `AcquisitionCardSheet.vue:54-62`. HV is now merged only under Auto HV (`autoHV`), with per-channel `hv` otherwise. Gain and offset are always in their own per-channel `gainOffset` column. The old merged shape is the one `acquisitionCardGain.test.ts` pins as the bug: differing offsets disappeared. A reader of the plan would rebuild it. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1976 — `scripts/judge/bugs.py:545` — `_not_judged`: when no verdict comes back, it reads `b.get("was") == "open"` on the merged bug, which is the lead only. `_merge` still falls back to the *first* carried member, so a group like [carried `was: unjudged`, carried `was: open`] gets a non-open lead. The merged bug then drops to `unjudged` and leaves the work list, even though one of its members was open. This is the same shape as the bug fixed here: a guard reads the lead while a different group member holds the property. `_queue_order` (bugs.py:646) already uses `any(... was == "open" ...)` across the group, which shows the property is meant to be group-wide. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1977 — `app/src/tasks/scheduler/task_record.jl:97` — `TaskRecord.output_name` contract comment. It still says the field is `task_output_name` and that `""` means "every run of it writes to the same place". After this change, both `run.jl` sites fill it from `task_params_name`, so a `writesInto` task now has a non-empty `output_name` (its input). The comment that defines the duplicate-run guard's key no longer matches what the field holds. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1977 — `docs/SCHEDULER.md:518` — this is the duplicate-run guard's contract, the same text the change just fixed in the `task_record.jl:97` comment. It still says the guard keys on "the same output name (`task_output_name`; `""` when the task names none)". Both `run.jl` sites (96, 153) now fill `output_name` from `task_params_name`, so a `writesInto` task such as tracking carries its input's name. Tracking B and tracking T therefore no longer collide. This doc text, the authoritative description of that key, now describes the old behaviour. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1982 — `docs/INSTALL.md:372` — the manual laptop tunnel recipe `ssh -L 8080:localhost:8080 <vm>` (and the `open "http://localhost:8080/api/auth?…"` line on 374). It has the same bug as the one this hunk fixes: the remote side is fixed at slot 0's 8080. On a VM shared by several OS users, a user on slot N tunnels to another user's Cecelia. The `api-token` they fetch is their own, so sign-in fails or reaches the wrong backend. It needs the same "read `api_port` from `~/.cecelia/cecelia.lock`" step, or a note about it. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1982 — `scripts/remote-launcher/cecelia-connect.sh:61` — the tunnel-reuse branch (`TUNNEL_UP=1` from `tunnel.pid` + `tunnel.port`). It has the same flaw as the hunk fixed: it trusts a remote port chosen earlier and never checks it against the lock. The new lock lookup sits only inside `if [ "$TUNNEL_UP" = "0" ]`. A tunnel built before Cecelia started (no lock, so it fell back to 8080), or by an older launcher, is reused on every later connect. `_port_open` only probes the local ssh listener, so it still answers when the remote side is another user's slot-0 backend. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1985 — `api/src/routes/claim.jl:190` — the `/api/claim/state` docstring still says "S0–S2 warnings as `### sNN` sections". [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1985 — `api/src/routes/claim.jl:37` — the header comment still says "The check's warnings are the entry's `### sNN · …` sections, so the analyst marks each one Good / Bad / Unsure". Same contract, now out of date. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1985 — `app/src/claim_checks.jl:536` — `_claim_merge_pops`. The diff changes the finding's `variable` sub from the raw key to `_claim_variable_label(k)`. The merge key groups on that sub (every sub except `pop`). The label drops information: `params.segment.cellposeMeasure.models.0.cellDiameter` and `…models.1.cellDiameter` both become "cellposeMeasure cellDiameter". Two such findings for the same pop land in one group, and since `length(pops) < 2` only `fs[1]` is kept, so the second difference disappears with no error. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1985 — `docs/API.md:168` — the reply contract for `/api/claim/checks` and `/api/claim/state` still reads `{findings, ran, skipped, notCompared, nRows, nUsed}` / `{ok, entryId, round, findings, ran, skipped}`. `claim_checks` now also returns `checks`, and `_claim_reply` adds `text`, `checks`, `review` and `closed`. The route comment in `routes/claim.jl` and the `api_claim_state` docstring were updated in the diff; the route index wasn't. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1985 — `docs/API.md:170` — the `/api/claim/state` row still says "each warning a `### sNN` section the analyst marks". Its reply shape also lacks `categories` and `categoryLabels`. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1985 — `docs/API.md:172` — the `/api/claim/review` row says "~\$1" (removed everywhere else in this diff) and the section format `### sNN · Review · …`. The format is now `### sNN · <Category> · text → action`. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1985 — `docs/inventory/FRONTEND.md:329` — the `claimApi.ts` inventory line still lists `stageCounts` and `skippedLine`, and describes `ClaimStrip.vue` as "stage chips via `InlineNote`, Review". This diff deletes those exports and the chip strip, and adds `findingsOf`, `openCount`, the popover and the checks list. The next discovery grep will point at helpers that no longer exist. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1985 — `mcp/cecelia_mcp/server.py:347` — the `state_claim` docstring still tells the model "the check_claim findings as its `### sNN` sections — each one the user marks good / bad / unsure". After this diff, check findings are plain lines and only review points are sections. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1991 — `app/src/tasks/chain/from_history.jl:79` — `_history_output`, the namespace lookup `string(get(params, k, "")) == name` reads only the current key. `task_output_name` now finds `name` through `formerKeys`, but this check still misses it. Saved params that carry only the former key (`clusterName` on the clustPops/clustTracks/clustRegions cluster specs) get the correct name with `ns = ""`. Its callers use `ns` for the replace warning (`app/src/tasks/chain/preflight.jl:71-73`, gated on `_preflight_fields(ns)`) and for history lineage (`app/src/tasks/chain/history_scope.jl:81`: `made`/`reads`/`_HP_RUN_NS`). In those cases they now act on a correct name with the wrong namespace. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #1999 — `api/src/sockets.jl:129` — `ws_status` → `record_task_outcome!`. This is where the API process records outcomes for frames relayed from the runner (`api/src/runner_api.jl` `task:status` branch). Same shape as the bug this diff fixes: the start is looked up by id, and a `task:restart` doesn't clear the mark. The new `_BANKED_START` mark is cleared only by `forget_task_start!`, and `_register_task!` (`app/src/tasks/scheduler/task_record.jl:178`) is its only caller in the runtime. Runner tasks are registered in the runner process, not the API process. So the API process never clears the mark, and `_live_run_under` there can't see the runner's run. Failing case: run A is cancelled and recorded. Its restart is cancelled while still queued. The runner's own row has `startedAt = ""`, so `ws_status` passes an empty `started_at`. `task_started_at` returns nothing, `_start_banked` is still true and the previous status was "cancelled", so the API inherits run A's start. That is exactly what the third new testset guards against, but here it happens on the relay path. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #2000 — `api/src/viewer_api.jl:615` — `try_serve_slab`'s `r = fetch(Threads.@spawn …)`: this has the same write-in-pool-task shape that this diff fixes with `_fetch_unwrapped`. When the client goes away, `_write_slab!` (`write(stream, body)`) throws the write IOError inside the spawned task. A plain `fetch` re-raises it wrapped in a `TaskFailedException`, so HTTP.jl logs that disconnect at Error and not Debug. The comment above it (lines ~611-613) says a failed write "propagates like any other handler error". Slab is the route most likely to hit this, because the viewer drops in-flight brick requests while scrubbing, pans and zooms. The fix would be to wrap it in `_fetch_unwrapped`. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #2005 — `frontend/src/modules/ViewerWindow.vue:1441` — `maskColourNote`, the trackclust branch. It still decides whether "a track cluster is drawn on this mask" with `tc.payload.pops.some(p => p.show !== false)`, but this diff now hides the mask's cluster pops by their point eye (`trackclustMaskLayer(tc.payload, (path, show) => popEyeOn(popEyes.value, vn, 'trackclust', path, show))`, :997). Two cases go wrong. If every eye is off while `show` is true, the mask is all grey and the note says nothing. If `show` is false and an eye is on, the mask is coloured and the note says "No track clusters on this mask". The pops branch next to it reads the rendered `maskTable`, not `show`. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #2016 — `mcp/cecelia_mcp/server.py:379` — the `get_task_history` tool docstring is a copy of the `/api/tasks/history` contract that this change rewrote in `docs/API.md` and `api/src/routes/image.jl:518`. The docstring still lists `runStatus` as only `done · failed · cancelled · interrupted · running`. It says nothing of `skipped`/`reason`, and it still says "`total` runs exist" and that `limit` cut the list. Under the new contract, `count` can be larger than `limit`, and `skipped` rows are counted in neither `total` nor `limit`. This docstring is what the observer reads, so it will treat `skipped` rows as unexplained runs. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #2021 — `frontend/src/lib/webgpu/brickVolumeRenderer.ts:646` — `lastImage.zLo` is never updated by `setZPlane`. The same file's `setZPlane` (line 1875) changes only `currentZLo`. If the label atlas then hits OOM, it rebuilds with `setImage(lastImage.meta, …, lastImage.zDepth, lastImage.zLo, true)`, which sets `currentZLo` back to the plane from the last full allocation. Brick fetches then load the old planes while the UI shows the new ones. This was already true for a single 2D plane. The diff now sends every ±n window step and every volume-mode window step through `setZPlane` instead of `reallocate`, so the stale `zLo` happens far more often. [**fixed_pre_commit**]

- #2023 — `app/src/analysis_runs.jl:213` — `_tracks_delete!` rebuilds the track measures by calling `TrackMeasures` with a hand-built params dict that carries only `valueName`, `forceRecompute` and `dims`. That is the same shape the hunk fixes: the rebuild doesn't match the params the existing table was measured with. The last measures run's `windowMinutes` (`track_measures.jl:22,30,480-484,542`, a spec param in `track_measures.json`) is dropped, so it falls back to 0. A table measured with tracklet windows would be rebuilt without them after a track-set delete. The call also goes to `_run_task` directly, without `resolve_run_params`/`validate_params`, which is the bypass the `execute.jl` hunk closes for incremental nodes. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #2030 — `api/src/routes/image.jl:257` — `api_import_register_legacy` calls `add_image!(s; …, meta=meta)`, and that `meta` has only the `legacy*` keys, no `createdBy`. - #2047 — `app/src/plotting/plot_data.jl:583` — the `raw` export branch for a count chart with no measure still builds its rows with `_population_metric_frame`. That function drops the `centroid_t` group and divides by the whole image's total `(uID, value_name)`, not by `(uID, value_name, t)`. So the board CSV export (`SummaryPanel.vue:824` → `getCsv` → `raw: true`) of a "Populations over time" plot writes one value per image instead of the per-frame proportions and zero frames on screen. - #2051 — `app/src/correction_staleness.jl:403` — `stale_outputs_for`: the `reason` string on `unknown` entries. The text assumes the only way to get `:unknown` is a run from before inputs were recorded. This round added a second way: `upstream` now returns `:unknown` when the read is pinned and its only logged writer, which did record its inputs, has gone stale. That is the new test case: smooth mints v2, then segmentation reads v1 pinned, then `default` v2 is replaced. The reader has `inputs`, so it gets "an input was made by a run from before inputs were recorded". That is false, because the smooth run recorded its inputs. Before this round, the case of several writers disagreeing already reached `:unknown` the same way. [**fixed_pre_commit**]
- #2052 — `app/src/run_log.jl:184` — `run_pop_reads` → `gated_pop_refs(r.params, lv)` is still called without `all_pickers`. This change makes `run_gates` count every population picker, on the grounds that a track analysis on `B/qc/_tracked` reads the cell gate `/qc`. `run_pop_reads` guards gate edits against the runs that depend on them, and it still skips any picker whose `accepts` lacks `live`. So a `clustTracks.cluster` run on `B/qc/_tracked` is stamped as reading `/qc` in `gates`, but it is not counted as a reader when someone edits `/qc`. [**fixed_pre_commit**]

- #2154 — `app/src/tasks/segment/branching_run.py:533` — `label_axes` / `_drop_pos` / `out_shape`. The shape is the same one this diff fixes in `measure_from_zarr`: the label store's axes are taken to be "image order minus C", with no check of the store's actual rank. When the input label set is a ridges store written as (T)YX for a single-plane image whose order still names Z, `_drop_pos` holds the *image's* Z position. That position actually points at Y in the store, so `out_shape` = `labels_level.shape` minus that position drops Y: you get (T, X), or (X,) without T. The (T)YX skeleton write into that store then fails. Branching on fibre labels is the natural next step after ridges. [**fixed_pre_commit**]

- #2224 — `api/src/sockets.jl:138` — `ws_status`, the relayed `running` branch: `note_task_started!(task_id, seed)` is first-write-wins, and the API server never calls `forget_task_start!` for a relayed restart. That reset happens only in the runner's own `_register_task!`. Same shape as this fix: a relay has no `TaskRecord`, so it cannot see a restart under the same id. Ordering: the old run's relayed `running` frame noted its start, and the restart's `running` frame is relayed before the old run's late terminal frame. The restart's frame then reads back the old start, so its live `startedAt` (line 147) shows the superseded run's start. After that, the old late frame finds `noted == started_at`, banks it and drops the note, so the new guard never triggers in this ordering. The fix only covers the case where the API server had no note for the old run. The comment above, "Seeding … is safe because it is first-write-wins" (sockets.jl:45), assumes ids are never reused. [**fixed_pre_commit**]

That map is only trimmed to the visible channels when the user clicks a role (`setChannelRole(…, roleChannels)`, `utils/channelRoles.ts:32`). - #1335 — `app/src/storage.jl:389` — `remove_image_version!`, the delete path for a value_name's store. When the entry is versioned (`{v1, v2, _latest}`, which the 9 converted pixel writers already produce), it gets a Dict rather than a filename, so `string(Dict)` matches no candidate path. It logs "not found on disk — clearing metadata only" and the commit drops the entry, which leaves every `vN` store orphaned on disk. - #1376 — `python/cecelia/utils/coastal_utils.py:832` — `CoastalUtils.predict_slice` → `_match_3d` → `coastal.utils.match_masks_3d`. This is a port of cellpose's `stitch3D` with the same flaw. When one plane is empty, `intersection_over_union(...)[1:, 1:]` comes back empty and the loop hits `continue` (`coastal/utils.py:169-170`). The next plane then keeps its raw per-plane ids 1..k, which are already in use on earlier planes. So plane 0's cell 1 and plane 2's cell 1 come back as one label, the same collision this diff fixes for cellpose. The coastal 3D output skips `split_z_gaps` entirely. One caveat on reusing it directly: coastal's pass 2, `_bridge_label_gaps` (gap_tolerance=1, `coastal/utils.py:199-200`), *deliberately* gives one label to runs separated by a single bad slice. So "a z-gap means a collision" is not exact here, and running `split_z_gaps` as-is would undo legitimate bridges. The collision also feeds `_bridge_label_gaps`' own `label_last_z` map. [**shipped_with_finding**]

If a plane is empty before the first match, its ids are reused rather than renumbered. The fix for this (`35fb7cf8`, `split_z_gaps`) is only on the unmerged `fix-cellpose-stitch-ids` branch, not on `main` or this branch. So on this branch only the coastal half of the 3D-stitch collision gets fixed, and cellpose 3D runs still give unrelated cells one label [**shipped_with_finding**]
